As a licensed contractor, Contractor is required to follow the law (B&P 7110(a.))
Pursuant to B&P 7159, you are entitled to receive a fully executed copy of the following
Contract before Contractor begins any work on your project. This should be in Bold 12-point
typeface.
Upon satisfactory payment being made for any portion of the work performed, the contractor,
prior to any further payment being made, will give you a full and unconditional release from
any potential mechanics lien for that portion of the work for which you have paid.
Any change to the services/materials listed in the contract must be executed by you and the
Contractor through a change order. “Extra Work and Change Orders become part of the
contract once the order is prepared in writing and signed by the parties prior to the
commencement of work covered by the new change order. The order must describe the scope of
the extra work or change, the cost to be added or subtracted from the contract, and the effect
the order will have on the schedule of progress payments.” (If the change will increase the cost
of the contract to the customer, you need to specify the approximate increase and what effect it
will have on the progress payments and completion date.)
In executing this Contract, you have the right to require the contractor to have a performance
and payment bond.

PRE-INSTALLATION ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS
Congratulations on purchasing your new project from Payless Kitchen & Bath! We wish to be out of your
way in the shortest possible time. Our goal is to have this experience be a positive one for you. Please
let us offer a few suggestions and with your cooperation we’ll have a successful project installation!
Please ensure the power is on for electrical tools and that sufficient lighting is available.
Keep animals and small children out of the work area. The installers cannot perform their duties
effectively with unnecessary interference.
Please remove all breakables such as glassware, lamps, mirrors, antiques, plants, special care items,
or those items dear to your heart from the unthinkable. Kindly remove all objects from shelves,
drawers, etc. Installers will remove and replace your furniture with the following exceptions:
computers, antiques, or excessively heavy furniture.
Please disengage the washer, dryer, ice maker, etc., if they are to be removed.
When installing flooring in your bedrooms, please strip the beds, and remove all items from floors
and closets.
Your inspection of the product and installation before the installer leaves the job area is highly
recommended and the Buyer will be considered to have accepted the product and installation after
the installer has left the job area. It is quicker and easier to correct small things when the installer is
still at your residence.
The progress payment or final payment must be available, and someone must be at the job site to
sign for receiving the merchandise, or work will not commence. In the case of financing, only a
signature is required, unless otherwise noted.
Protecting your investment: Proper care and maintenance of your new remodeling project will
ensure years of beautiful use and enjoyment. Please visit our website for the most updated
maintenance instructions.
_________________
Signature
09/04/24
_________________
Date

California Home Improvement Agreement
Information About the Contractor's State License Board (CSLB)
CSLB is the state consumer protection agency that licenses and regulates construction
contractors.
Contact CSLB for information about the licensed contractor you are considering, including
information about disclosable complaints, disciplinary actions, and civil judgments that are
reported to CSLB.
Use only licensed contractors. If you file a complaint against a licensed contractor within the
legal deadline (usually four years), CSLB has authority to investigate the complaint. If you use
an unlicensed contractor, CSLB may not be able to help you resolve your complaint. Your only
remedy may be in civil court, and you may be liable for damages arising out of any injuries to
the unlicensed contractor or the unlicensed contractor's employees.
For more information:

California Home Improvement Agreement
Mechanics' Lien Warning
Anyone who helps improve your property, but who is not paid, may record what is called a
mechanics' lien on your property. A mechanics' lien is a claim, like a mortgage or home equity
loan, made against your property and recorded with the county recorder.
Even if you pay your contractor in full, unpaid subcontractors, suppliers, and laborers who
helped to improve your property may record mechanics' liens and sue you in court to
foreclose the lien. If a court finds the lien is valid, you could be forced to pay twice or have a
court officer sell your home to pay the lien. Liens can also affect your credit.
To preserve their right to record a lien, each subcontractor and material supplier must provide
you with a document called a '20-Day Preliminary Notice'. This notice is not a lien. The
purpose of the notice is to let you know that the person who sends you the notice has the
right to record a lien on your property if he or she is not paid.
BE CAREFUL. The Preliminary Notice can be sent up to 20 days after the subcontractor starts
work or the supplier provides material. This can be a big problem if you pay your contractor
before you have received the Preliminary Notices. You will not get Preliminary Notices from
your prime contractor or from laborers who work on your project. The law assumes that you
already know they are improving your property.
PROTECT YOURSELF FROM LIENS. You can protect yourself from liens by getting a list from
your contractor of all the subcontractors and material suppliers that work on your project.
Find out from your contractor when these subcontractors started work and when these
suppliers delivered goods or materials. Then wait 20 days, paying attention to the Preliminary
Notices you receive.
PAY WITH JOINT CHECKS. One way to protect yourself is to pay with a joint check. When your
contractor tells you it is time to pay for the work of a subcontractor or supplier who has
provided you with a Preliminary Notice, write a joint check payable to both the contractor and
the subcontractor or material supplier.
For other ways to prevent liens, visit CSLB's Web site at www.cslb.ca.gov or call CSLB at 800-
321- CSLB (2752).
REMEMBER, IF YOU DO NOTHING, YOU RISK HAVING A LIEN PLACED ON YOUR HOME. This can
mean that you may have to pay twice, or face the forced sale of your home to pay what you
owe.
You are entitled to a completely filled in copy of this agreement, signed by both you and the
contractor, before any work may be started. The law requires that the contractor give you a
notice explaining your right to cancel.
(Client’s Initials)
Carpet Wagon Glendale_ Inc. Payless Kitchen Cabinets & Bath Makeover - Home Improvement Agreement - Copy of 1791341 _HB_ - Page 13 of 32

California Home Improvement Agreement
Notice of Three-Day Right to Cancel
Date of Transaction: 09/04/24
You, the buyer, have the right to cancel this contract within three business days. You may
cancel by emailing, mailing, faxing, or delivering a written notice to the contractor at the
contractor's place of business by midnight of the third business day after you received a
signed and dated copy of the contract that includes this notice. Include your name, your
address, and the date you received the signed copy of the contract and this notice.
If you cancel, the contractor must return to you anything you paid within 10 days of receiving
the notice of cancellation. For your part, you must make available to the contractor at your
residence, in substantially as good condition as you received it, any goods delivered to you
under this contract or sale. Or, you may, if you wish, comply with the contractor's instructions
on how to return the goods at the contractor's expense and risk. If you do make the goods
available to the contractor and the contractor does not pick them up within 20 days of the
date of your notice of cancellation, you may keep them without any further obligation. If you
fail to make the goods available to the contractor, or if you agree to return the goods to the
contractor and fail to do so, then you remain liable for performance of all obligations under
the contract.
To cancel this transaction, mail or deliver a signed and dated copy of the Notice of
Cancellation, or any other written notice to:
Contractor Name: CWGI dba PAYLESS KITCHEN CABINETS & BATH MAKEOVER Address: 3614
SAN FERNANDO RD., GLENDALE, CA 91204 not later than midnight of THREE BUSINESS DAYS
OF THE DATE OF TRANSACTION California Business and Professions Code Section § 7159
requires that this form be signed and dated when the contract is signed.

and “fifth,” respectively, for a buyer who is a senior citizen (65 years of age or older).

California Home Improvement Agreement
FURTHER DISCLOSURES
1.General Terms and Conditions. This Agreement will become binding only when approved
by a Corporate officer of Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc., dba Payless Kitchen Cabinets and
Bath Makeover (CWGI) License# 913187. If the proposal is rejected, Buyer will be notified in
writing, via email within five (5) days of rejection. Any deposits received will be refunded
within thirty (30) days of notice of rejection. In the event of default or breach by buyer or if
Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc, dba Payless Kitchen Cabinets and Bath Makeover (CWGI)
deems itself insecure, CWGI may cancel the contract and buyer's sole remedy shall be a
refund of sums advanced for work not then completed.
2.Processing. All processing orders are done through the Corporate office information listed
on this contract. Materials on this invoice are to be ordered as per Buyer's selection of
quality, color, and dimensions. Since materials are special ordered or cut from stock per the
custom design, this Agreement is not subject to cancellation or change.
3. Performance. Unless otherwise written, CWGI will immediately perform this agreement
with due diligence. Any material returned to stock will incur a 25% restocking charge plus
any applicable shipping charges.
4.Cancellation or Termination.
You have the right to cancel or terminate this Agreement at various stages:
A. Three / Five-Day Right to Cancel - See above “Notices” and Attachments for more
information and important notices. Five-Day Right to Cancel for Senior Citizens (65 years
of age or older); Three-Day Right to Cancel for All Other Customers.
B. Termination - At any time after the Three / Five-Day Cancellation period by providing
written notice via one or more methods: mailed or hand-delivered to Carpet Wagon
Glendale Inc (CWGI), 3614 San Fernando Rd., Glendale, CA 91204, with confirmation
email. Verbal notices, phone calls, or texts, are not effective to terminate this
Agreement.
If you Cancel within the Three / Five-day period provided above, any deposit(s) or other
payment(s) made by you will be returned within ten (10) business days after CWGIs receipt of
your notice of cancellation.
If paid by credit card, your refund will be made to the card used.
If paid through a financed transaction, the refund will be issued to the finance company.
If paid by cash or check, your refund will be made by a company check.
If you cancel, you must make available to CWGI at your Home, in substantially as good
condition as when received, any goods delivered to you under this Agreement. If you fail to
make the goods available to CWGI, or if you agree to return the goods and fail to do so, you
will remain liable for the performance of all obligations under the Agreement.

In the event of termination, you agree to pay CWGI (1) 33% of the Contract Price, plus (2) the

on Buyer’s real property, Buyer agrees to pay an additional charge equal to the cost of

California Home Improvement Agreement
expenses of local municipalities are assessed to the work performed or being performed

14.Dust. As with any type of construction project, dust may be created. We encourage our
clients to dust proof as much as possible. We do not clean up dust created in the work
process, but we will try to minimize its production as best as we can.
15.Wall Repair. If, during the installation phase, we are required to perform unforeseen
repairs to your existing walls, an additional charge of time and material will apply.
16.Existing Plumbing. We are not responsible for inspecting, servicing, or modifying your
existing plumbing. If, during the installation of Contractor's products (acrylic/tile/quartz),
we determine that the existing plumbing is deficient or needs repair or adjustments, we
will notify you as soon as reasonably practical. If the plumbing repairs or modifications are
needed, you may need to have the plumbing corrected before installation can be
completed. In addition, because the existing plumbing may be old, corroded, or in need of
repair or replacement, we are not liable for any damage to plumbing below or behind the
tub, including shut-off valves and drain shoes.
17.Demolition. Unless specified and charged for on this agreement, we are not responsible
for the removal or reinstallation of any appliances. All gas and water lines need to be shut
off prior to our arrival. Our demolition crew will remove all cabinets, countertops, and
backsplashes. All trash will be taken by the crew after completion of demolition. If Buyer
requests us to remove appliances, we will not be responsible for any damage occurred.
18.Natural Products. Wood is natural product with natural differences in texture, tone, and
grain. A slight variation of any of these is to be expected from hardwood plank to plank,
cabinet door to door, and cabinet color glaze or stain. We do not warrant against these
natural anomalies, which can be found in various flooring and kitchen cabinet products. To
achieve a uniform appearance on wood flooring across the entire floor, we mix several
planks from different cartons, maximizing aesthetic appearance and color consistency.
19.Granite & Stone Countertops. All granite countertops (prefabricated or custom) are
natural stone. No two slabs are alike and some granite countertops have natural veins,
which may appear as cracks to the eye. Contractor cannot make any claims nor guarantees
that a specific slab, or part of a slab, from your selection will be used or avoided in a
particular area of your installation. Natural stone is subject to inherent variation in color,
veining, and dimensions. Natural stone's mineral characteristics create a visible texture
within the stone and are to be expected in the finished product. Pitting, dry veins, and
fissures can all be part of the stone.
20.Uneven Walls. CWGI cannot be held responsible for uneven walls and ceilings, visible gaps

California Home Improvement Agreement
between crown molding to ceilings, and cabinets installed next to walls. These will not be
filled in by our installers. We recommend you hire a handyman or contractor if these issues
exist.
21.Water Heater Hookup and Plumbing. Unless specified and charged for on this agreement,
we do not connect gas appliances or plumbing. We do not warrant plumbing service from
leaks or household damage resulting from leaks. You should have a professional plumber
perform this type of work, including water and drain hookup, unless specified and charged
for in this agreement.
22.Sub-Floor Preparation. Prior to the installation of our new flooring, the installer must
prepare your sub-floor, especially in hardwood, tile, luxury vinyl, and laminate installation.
In all estimates and bids, floor preparation is not included. Our sales associates do not
have the proper equipment to determine appropriate actions for your sub-floor. All floor
preparation has an additional cost.
23.Wires. Sometimes wires are canceled under flooring. They could be an alarm, phone
speaker, or similar wires, which may interfere with installation. It is recommended that
you call a professional to reroute lines after our installation is complete. If lines are in the
way of our installation, we cannot guarantee that they will continue to work after the
installation of the new flooring, since wires could be pierced, pinched, or stretched by
flooring product or nails.
24.Door Trimming. Please note that sometimes new floor installed may be higher than old
flooring, and in some cases, doors may need to be removed and trimmed to make
clearance for new flooring. Unless specified and charged for on this agreement, there is an
additional charge for this service.
25.Moving Large Items. We do not move any item that requires more than two individuals,
weigh an excess of 250 pounds, or are awkward in shape or oversized. Furniture that is too
large to be moved needs to be disassembled in order for us to move it. Unless specified
and charged for on this agreement, please arrange to have these items ready for removal
or to have them moved away from the work area before our crew arrives.
26.Work Not Part of Agreement. CWGI is not responsible for existing structural defects, dry
rot, or code violations at the service address. No repairing, drywall, painting, plumbing,
electrical, carpentry, trash hauling, wallpapering, or decorating is included unless specified
and charged for in this agreement. Mold remediation, asbestos abatement, replacing
rotted or termite damaged wood, hidden damages, frame or subfloor repair, relocating
repairing or adding plumbing or electrical, adding ducting/venting, painting, knocking
down walls, permit fees, bringing house up to code, flooring, glass door, vanity, countertop
sink faucet are not part of this agreement unless specified and charged for in this
agreement.

California Home Improvement Agreement
27.Buyer’s Obligations. Buyer has the obligation and responsibility to make any minor
adjustments, such as hinge adjustments, screw tightening, and re-caulking of sealant on
walls and sinks. If specifications call for re-use of existing appliances or plumbing fixtures,
no responsibility shall be implied on the part of Payless Kitchen Cabinet for functioning or
service. Code upgrades that may be required due to specific city codes, not specified here,
are not included in this contract.
28.Cooperation. In the event of dissatisfaction of Buyer with materials, labor or installation
date, Buyer agrees to cooperate with Contractor and allow reasonable length of time for
completion of corrective measures. Text and email communication may not be fluid/
effective and tone may be misunderstood. Buyer agrees to communicate over the phone.
We offer options like FaceTime, Zoom, Skype, Face to Face and other communication
methods as well.
29.Warranty Remedy. The warranty does not cover adjustments required due to settling of
your home. We do not cover change in color or damage to the cabinet finish due to
exposure to environmental factors such as natural and artificial light or moisture. Due to
the natural variations in wood grains, the possibility of tone and shading differences from
piece to piece is normal and is not considered defective.
30.Warranty. At Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc., dba Payless Kitchen Cabinets and Bath
Makeover (CWGI), we are committed to delivering the highest quality remodeling products
and ensuring your satisfaction. We stand behind all our Signature Series, Designer Series,
BCI, and Sammuel Mueller products for craftsmanship and materials, and we are pleased
to offer you our Limited Lifetime Product Warranty, to provide peace of mind for years to
come. Refer to manufacturer warranty for all other products. All material and product
warranties are the responsibility of the manufacturers. Manufacturer will be solely
responsible for their original warranty. Payless Kitchen & Bath shall only be responsible for
any warranty extensions after, and only after, the original manufacturer warranty has
expired. For all other products that offer less than a lifetime warranty, we offer a pro-rated
warranty for any product defects. The total contract price will be divided by the length of
the product warranty to determine an annual rate. This rate will be multiplied by the
number of years you have used the product to calculate the usage value for offset
purposes. In the unlikely event of a product defect, we will work diligently to address any
issues. For product defects or replacements that occur after the initial 2-year period, you
will be responsible for labor costs associated with repairs or replacements.

A Limited Lifetime Labor Guarantee on the installation work performed by CWGI, which
warranties that workmanship will be free of defects. If you require repairs for installation-

This warranty does not cover routine maintenance like caulking or cleaning. This warranty

writing by a CWGI officer. No other warranty, express or implied, is provided by CWGI. We are
not liable for any incidental or consequential damages, whether arising from contract, tort, or
otherwise.
This warranty becomes effective only after the full contract price, including any additional

UNDER NO CIRCUMSTANCES WILL CWGI BE LIABLE FOR INCIDENTAL, INDIRECT, SPECIAL,

YOU ARE IN THE PROCESS OF SIGNING A LEGALLY BINDING AGREEMENT. BEFORE DOING

As a licensed contractor, Contractor is required to follow the law (B&P 7110(a.))
Pursuant to B&P 7159, you are entitled to receive a fully executed copy of the following Contract before
Contractor begins any work on your project. This should be in Bold 12-point typeface.
Upon satisfactory payment being made for any portion of the work performed, the contractor, prior to any
further payment being made, will give you a full and unconditional release from any potential mechanics lien
for that portion of the work for which you have paid.
Any change to the services/materials listed in the contract must be executed by you and the Contractor through
a change order. “Extra Work and Change Orders become part of the contract once the order is prepared in
writing and signed by the parties prior to the commencement of work covered by the new change order. The
order must describe the scope of the extra work or change, the cost to be added or subtracted from the
contract, and the effect the order will have on the schedule of progress payments.” (If the change will increase
the cost of the contract to the customer, you need to specify the approximate increase and what effect it will
have on the progress payments and completion date.)
In executing this Contract, you have the right to require the contractor to have a performance and payment
bond.

California Home Improvement Agreement
PRE-INSTALLATION ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS
Congratulations on purchasing your new project from Payless Kitchen & Bath! We wish to be out of your way in the
shortest possible time. Our goal is to have this experience be a positive one for you. Please let us offer a few
suggestions and with your cooperation we’ll have a successful project installation!
Please ensure the power is on for electrical tools and that sufficient lighting is available.
Keep animals and small children out of the work area. The installers cannot perform their duties effectively
with unnecessary interference.
Please remove all breakables such as glassware, lamps, mirrors, antiques, plants, special care items, or
those items dear to your heart from the unthinkable. Kindly remove all objects from shelves, drawers, etc.
Installers will remove and replace your furniture with the following exceptions: computers, antiques, or
excessively heavy furniture.
Please disengage the washer, dryer, ice maker, etc., if they are to be removed.
When installing flooring in your bedrooms, please strip the beds, and remove all items from floors and
closets.
Your inspection of the product and installation before the installer leaves the job area is highly
recommended and the Buyer will be considered to have accepted the product and installation after the
installer has left the job area. It is quicker and easier to correct small things when the installer is still at your
residence.
The progress payment or final payment must be available, and someone must be at the job site to sign for
receiving the merchandise, or work will not commence. In the case of financing, only a signature is
required, unless otherwise noted.
Protecting your investment: Proper care and maintenance of your new remodeling project will ensure years
of beautiful use and enjoyment. Please visit our website for the most updated maintenance instructions.
01/10/26
Signature Date

California Home Improvement Agreement
Information About the Contractor's State License Board (CSLB)
CSLB is the state consumer protection agency that licenses and regulates construction contractors.
Contact CSLB for information about the licensed contractor you are considering, including information about
disclosable complaints, disciplinary actions, and civil judgments that are reported to CSLB.
Use only licensed contractors. If you file a complaint against a licensed contractor within the legal deadline
(usually four years), CSLB has authority to investigate the complaint. If you use an unlicensed contractor,
CSLB may not be able to help you resolve your complaint. Your only remedy may be in civil court, and you
may be liable for damages arising out of any injuries to the unlicensed contractor or the unlicensed
contractor's employees.
For more information:
Visit CSLB's Internet Web site at www.cslb.ca.gov
Call CSLB at 800-321-CSLB (2752)
Write CSLB at P.O. Box 26000, Sacramento, CA 95826

California Home Improvement Agreement
Mechanics' Lien Warning
Anyone who helps improve your property, but who is not paid, may record what is called a mechanics' lien
on your property. A mechanics' lien is a claim, like a mortgage or home equity loan, made against your
property and recorded with the county recorder.
Even if you pay your contractor in full, unpaid subcontractors, suppliers, and laborers who helped to improve
your property may record mechanics' liens and sue you in court to foreclose the lien. If a court finds the lien
is valid, you could be forced to pay twice or have a court officer sell your home to pay the lien. Liens can also
affect your credit.
To preserve their right to record a lien, each subcontractor and material supplier must provide you with a
document called a '20-Day Preliminary Notice'. This notice is not a lien. The purpose of the notice is to let
you know that the person who sends you the notice has the right to record a lien on your property if he or
she is not paid.
BE CAREFUL. The Preliminary Notice can be sent up to 20 days after the subcontractor starts work or the
supplier provides material. This can be a big problem if you pay your contractor before you have received
the Preliminary Notices. You will not get Preliminary Notices from your prime contractor or from laborers
who work on your project. The law assumes that you already know they are improving your property.
PROTECT YOURSELF FROM LIENS. You can protect yourself from liens by getting a list from your contractor
of all the subcontractors and material suppliers that work on your project. Find out from your contractor
when these subcontractors started work and when these suppliers delivered goods or materials. Then wait
20 days, paying attention to the Preliminary Notices you receive.
PAY WITH JOINT CHECKS. One way to protect yourself is to pay with a joint check. When your contractor
tells you it is time to pay for the work of a subcontractor or supplier who has provided you with a
Preliminary Notice, write a joint check payable to both the contractor and the subcontractor or material
supplier.
For other ways to prevent liens, visit CSLB's Web site at www.cslb.ca.gov or call CSLB at 800- 321- CSLB
(2752).
REMEMBER, IF YOU DO NOTHING, YOU RISK HAVING A LIEN PLACED ON YOUR HOME. This can mean that you
may have to pay twice, or face the forced sale of your home to pay what you owe.
You are entitled to a completely filled in copy of this agreement, signed by both you and the contractor,
before any work may be started. The law requires that the contractor give you a notice explaining your
right to cancel.
(Client’s Initials)
Carpet Wagon Glendale_ Inc. Payless Kitchen Cabinets & Bath Makeover - Home Improvement Agreement - 2527258 - Page 15 of 28

California Home Improvement Agreement
Notice of Three-Day Right to Cancel
Date of Transaction: 01/10/2026
You, the buyer, have the right to cancel this contract within three business days. You may cancel by emailing,
mailing, faxing, or delivering a written notice to the contractor at the contractor's place of business by
midnight of the third business day after you received a signed and dated copy of the contract that includes
this notice. Include your name, your address, and the date you received the signed copy of the contract and
this notice.
If you cancel, the contractor must return to you anything you paid within 10 days of receiving the notice of
cancellation. For your part, you must make available to the contractor at your residence, in substantially as
good condition as you received it, any goods delivered to you under this contract or sale. Or, you may, if you
wish, comply with the contractor's instructions on how to return the goods at the contractor's expense and
risk. If you do make the goods available to the contractor and the contractor does not pick them up within
20 days of the date of your notice of cancellation, you may keep them without any further obligation. If you
fail to make the goods available to the contractor, or if you agree to return the goods to the contractor and
fail to do so, then you remain liable for performance of all obligations under the contract.
To cancel this transaction, mail or deliver a signed and dated copy of the Notice of Cancellation, or any other
written notice to:
Contractor Name: CWGI dba PAYLESS KITCHEN CABINETS & BATH MAKEOVER Address: 3614 SAN FERNANDO
RD., GLENDALE, CA 91204 not later than midnight of THREE BUSINESS DAYS OF THE DATE OF TRANSACTION
California Business and Professions Code Section § 7159 requires that this form be signed and dated when

California Home Improvement Agreement
FURTHER DISCLOSURES
1.General Terms and Conditions. This Agreement will become binding only when approved by a
Corporate officer of Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc., dba Payless Kitchen Cabinets and Bath Makeover
(CWGI) License# 913187. If the proposal is rejected, Buyer will be notified in writing, via email within
five (5) days of rejection. Any deposits received will be refunded within thirty (30) days of notice of
rejection. In the event of default or breach by buyer or if Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc, dba Payless
Kitchen Cabinets and Bath Makeover (CWGI) deems itself insecure, CWGI may cancel the contract and
buyer's sole remedy shall be a refund of sums advanced for work not then completed.
2.Processing. All processing orders are done through the Corporate office information listed on this
contract. Materials on this invoice are to be ordered as per Buyer's selection of quality, color, and
dimensions. Since materials are special ordered or cut from stock per the custom design, this
Agreement is not subject to cancellation or change.
3.Performance. Unless otherwise written, CWGI will immediately perform this agreement with due
diligence. Any material returned to stock will incur a 25% restocking charge plus any applicable shipping
charges.
4.Written & Documented Change Orders. Any modification to the scope of work must be made through a
written and signed Change Order that includes any changes to job scope, cost, and adjustments to
completion time if needed.
5.Cancellation or Termination. Any modification to the scope of work must be made through a written
and signed Change Order that includes any changes to job scope, cost, and adjustments to completion
time if needed. You have the right to cancel or terminate this Agreement at various stages:
A. Three / Five-Day Right to Cancel - See above “Notices” and Attachments for more information and
important notices. Five-Day Right to Cancel for Senior Citizens (65 years of age or older); Three-Day
Right to Cancel for All Other Customers.
B. Termination - At any time after the Three / Five-Day Cancellation period by providing written notice
via one or more methods: mailed or hand-delivered to Carpet Wagon Glendale Inc (CWGI), 3614 San
Fernando Rd., Glendale, CA 91204, with confirmation email. Verbal notices, phone calls, or texts, are
not effective to terminate this Agreement.
If you Cancel within the Three/Five-day period provided above, any deposit(s) or other payment(s)
made by you will be returned within ten (10) business days after CWGIs receipt of your notice of
cancellation.
If paid by credit card, your refund will be made to the card used.
If paid through a financed transaction, the refund will be issued to the finance company.
If paid by cash or check, your refund will be made by a company check.

California Home Improvement Agreement
If you cancel, you must make available to CWGI at your Home, in substantially as good condition as
when received, any goods delivered to you under this Agreement. If you fail to make the goods available
to CWGI, or if you agree to return the goods and fail to do so, you will remain liable for the performance
of all obligations under the Agreement.
In the event of termination, you agree to pay CWGI (1) 33% of the Contract Price, plus (2) the actual
cost to CWGI for labor, labor burden, materials, products (including special or custom order products),
equipment, administrative and marketing costs, site measure, any other amounts set forth in this
Agreement, together with an overhead mark-up of 25% on actual expenses incurred through the date of
termination. Without limiting CWGI’s other remedies, CWGI may withhold all amounts that you owe to
CWGI from the down payment, other payments you have made, and any other payments made on your
behalf from the financing company you chose. Any net refunds due to the Customer will be made within
thirty (30) business days thereafter. Termination of the Agreement with CWGI does not terminate your
continuing obligation(s) to make payment for any previously authorized Credit Card Transactions or
Financed Transactions. You agree to not dispute, reject, or request a reversal of any charges previously
paid to CWGI by Credit Card. In the event your credit card company or financing company cancels,
reverses, or otherwise rescinds any payments made to CWGI on your behalf, you agree to immediately
repay to CWGI all such amounts.
6.Payment Terms. If Buyer does not pay the sums of money as and when they become due, a late charge
of one and one-half percent (1.5%) for each month, or part of each month, following the invoice date
during which the invoice remains will be added to the remaining balance.
7.Payment in Full. Payment in full is due immediately upon the completion of the installation. Minor
touch-ups, adjustments, punch list items, repairs, and warranty service work, will not hold up
completion payment. CWGI is not responsible for “Client Supplied” materials that arrive
damaged/incorrect/or late and CWGI still requires payment in full unless CWGI supplied the
late/damaged materials. Any stop payment of check or credit card payment will result in a standard
service fee, plus any bank fees charged to CWGI.
8.Title to Goods. Title to Goods on this order remains with CWGI until paid for by Buyer, and said
merchandise remains and shall be considered personal property until the full amount due is paid. If any
payment of the above is not made when due, CWGI shall have the right to repossess the
aforementioned property. If CWGI is obligated to retain an attorney to collect, and/or CWGI is obligated
to place a Mechanic's Lien on the Buyer's property, Buyer agrees to pay all fees and costs pertaining to
the matter. Interest will be charged at the legally permissible rate on past due accounts. In the event of
default in the payment of any amount due, and if this account is placed in the hands of an agency or
attorney for collection or legal action including but not limited to the recordation of a Mechanic's Lien
on Buyer’s real property, Buyer agrees to pay an additional charge equal to the cost of collection
including agency and attorney fees, release of Mechanic's Lien, and all court costs incurred and
permitted by the laws governing these transactions.
9.Substantial Completion. CWGI will conduct a final walkthrough with the homeowner using a
standardized checklist to ensure all contracted items are complete and meet client expectations before
final payment is requested. Minor touch-ups and adjustments will not hold up completion payment.

California Home Improvement Agreement
10.Arbitration Agreement. With this agreement, any claim, dispute, or controversy arising out of, relating
to, or concerning the project contract or this agreement, shall be decided by binding arbitration through
the, then-existing, arbitration program of the Los Angeles Bar Association and in accordance with the
then-existing Rules of that organization. Any such arbitration proceedings shall be brought and held in
the county of Los Angeles. The decision of the arbitrator shall be binding and conclusive upon all parties
involved and judgment upon any award of the arbitration may be entered by any court having
competent jurisdiction. This provision shall be specifically enforceable in any court of competent
jurisdiction. You agree that CWGI may at any time and without notice change the terms, conditions and
notices under which this contract is offered.
11. Attorney’s Fees. In the event of any dispute or litigation with respect to this contract, the prevailing
party shall be entitled to recover its reasonable costs thereof, including attorney’s fees. No action for
breach of this contract shall be commenced by Buyer, except within two years after the completion or
cessation of work under this Agreement. This limitation applies to all actions of any character, whether
at law or in equity, and whether in contract, tort or otherwise.
12.Cancellation for Non-Payment. CWGI may cancel this contract if Buyer does not allow CWGI to perform
on this contract due to reasons beyond CWGI’s control. If CWGI has to cancel, Buyer shall be responsible
for any and all costs for work produced or performed to date, plus restocking, shipping, administrative,
and commission fees and costs. Because CWGI products are custom designed, we cannot refund your
deposit after the rescission period has expired.
13. Excusable Delay and Uncontrollable Factors. CWGI is not responsible for start or finish delays resulting
from delays beyond our control including, but not limited to, acts of nature (rain, calamity),
governmental actions, manufacturing or delivery delays, damage to merchandise caused by third
parties, labor strikes or unrest, buyer’s credit or financing, incorrect information provided by Buyer,
legal encumbrances on your property, your property’s nonconformance with zoning requirements,
hidden or unforeseen physical or hazardous conditions (including, but not limited to, environmental
hazards like mold, asbestos, and lead paint) at your service address, or your non-compliance with this
agreement. CWGI reserves the right to terminate this agreement or discontinue installation given any of
the foregoing conditions. CWGI, cannot be held responsible for such uncontrollable factors.
14.Estimated Dates. CWGI guarantees timely project execution. If the project is delayed due to causes
within CWGI’s control (excluding acts of nature, permitting delays, or client-related changes), a
compensation of $50 per day, up to a maximum of $500, will be provided upon client request. Buyer
understands that any delivery or installation date is merely a best estimate of when performance will
begin, and that performance may be subject to delays. CWGI’s failure to perform this agreement by
given delivery or installation date shall not be a breach of this agreement, and CWGI shall not be liable
for damage to Buyer for any delay in performance of this agreement. Final payment is not due until a
client signs off on a completed final walkthrough checklist confirming that the scope of work has been
completed to their satisfaction.
15.Permits and Fees. In the event that special, or required permits, fees, bonds, or other expenses of local
municipalities are assessed to the work performed or being performed under this agreement, it shall be
the responsibility of the Buyer and will be added to the balance due under this agreement.

California Home Improvement Agreement
16.Dust. As with any type of construction project, dust may be created. We encourage our clients to dust
proof as much as possible. We do not clean up dust created in the work process, but we will try to
minimize its production as best as we can.
17.Wall Repair. If, during the installation phase, we are required to perform unforeseen repairs to your
existing walls, an additional charge of time and material will apply.
18.Existing Plumbing. We are not responsible for inspecting, servicing, or modifying your existing
plumbing. If, during the installation of Contractor's products (acrylic/tile/quartz), we determine that the
existing plumbing is deficient or needs repair or adjustments, we will notify you as soon as reasonably
practical. If the plumbing repairs or modifications are needed, you may need to have the plumbing
corrected before installation can be completed. In addition, because the existing plumbing may be old,
corroded, or in need of repair or replacement, we are not liable for any damage to plumbing below or
behind the tub, including shut-off valves and drain shoes.
19.Demolition. Unless specified and charged for on this agreement, we are not responsible for the removal
or reinstallation of any appliances. All gas and water lines need to be shut off prior to our arrival. Our
demolition crew will remove all cabinets, countertops, and backsplashes. All trash will be taken by the
crew after completion of demolition. If Buyer requests us to remove appliances, we will not be
responsible for any damage occurred.
20.Natural Products. Wood is natural product with natural differences in texture, tone, and grain. A slight
variation of any of these is to be expected from hardwood plank to plank, cabinet door to door, and
cabinet color glaze or stain. We do not warrant against these natural anomalies, which can be found in
various flooring and kitchen cabinet products. To achieve a uniform appearance on wood flooring across
the entire floor, we mix several planks from different cartons, maximizing aesthetic appearance and
color consistency.
21.Granite & Stone Countertops. All granite countertops (prefabricated or custom) are natural stone. No
two slabs are alike and some granite countertops have natural veins, which may appear as cracks to the
eye. Contractor cannot make any claims nor guarantees that a specific slab, or part of a slab, from your
selection will be used or avoided in a particular area of your installation. Natural stone is subject to
inherent variation in color, veining, and dimensions. Natural stone's mineral characteristics create a
visible texture within the stone and are to be expected in the finished product. Pitting, dry veins, and
fissures can all be part of the stone.
22.Uneven Walls. CWGI cannot be held responsible for uneven walls and ceilings, visible gaps between
crown molding to ceilings, and cabinets installed next to walls. These will not be filled in by our
installers. We recommend you hire a handyman or contractor if these issues exist.
23.Water Heater Hookup and Plumbing. Unless specified and charged for on this agreement, we do not
connect gas appliances or plumbing. We do not warrant plumbing service from leaks or household
damage resulting from leaks. You should have a professional plumber perform this type of work,
including water and drain hookup, unless specified and charged for in this agreement.

California Home Improvement Agreement
24.Sub-Floor Preparation. Prior to the installation of our new flooring, the installer must prepare your sub-
floor, especially in hardwood, tile, luxury vinyl, and laminate installation. In all estimates and bids, floor
preparation is not included. Our sales associates do not have the proper equipment to determine
appropriate actions for your sub-floor. All floor preparation has an additional cost.
25.Wires. Sometimes wires are canceled under flooring. They could be an alarm, phone speaker, or similar
wires, which may interfere with installation. It is recommended that you call a professional to reroute
lines after our installation is complete. If lines are in the way of our installation, we cannot guarantee
that they will continue to work after the installation of the new flooring, since wires could be pierced,
pinched, or stretched by flooring product or nails.
26.Door Trimming. Please note that sometimes new floor installed may be higher than old flooring, and in
some cases, doors may need to be removed and trimmed to make clearance for new flooring. Unless
specified and charged for on this agreement, there is an additional charge for this service.
27.Moving Large Items. We do not move any item that requires more than two individuals, weigh an
excess of 250 pounds, or are awkward in shape or oversized. Furniture that is too large to be moved
needs to be disassembled in order for us to move it. Unless specified and charged for on this
agreement, please arrange to have these items ready for removal or to have them moved away from the
work area before our crew arrives.
28.Work Not Part of Agreement. CWGI is not responsible for existing structural defects, dry rot, or code
violations at the service address. No repairing, drywall, painting, plumbing, electrical, carpentry, trash
hauling, wallpapering, or decorating is included unless specified and charged for in this agreement.
Mold remediation, asbestos abatement, replacing rotted or termite damaged wood, hidden damages,
frame or subfloor repair, relocating repairing or adding plumbing or electrical, adding ducting/venting,
painting, knocking down walls, permit fees, bringing house up to code, flooring, glass door, vanity,
countertop sink faucet are not part of this agreement unless specified and charged for in this
agreement.
29.Buyer’s Obligations. Buyer has the obligation and responsibility to make any minor adjustments, such as
hinge adjustments, screw tightening, and re-caulking of sealant on walls and sinks. If specifications call
for re-use of existing appliances or plumbing fixtures, no responsibility shall be implied on the part of
Payless Kitchen Cabinet for functioning or service. Code upgrades that may be required due to specific
city codes, not specified here, are not included in this contract.
30.Cooperation. In the event of dissatisfaction of Buyer with materials, labor or installation date, Buyer
agrees to cooperate with Contractor and allow reasonable length of time for completion of corrective
measures. Text and email communication may not be fluid/effective and tone may be misunderstood.
Buyer agrees to communicate over the phone. We offer options like FaceTime, Zoom, Skype, Face to
Face and other communication methods as well.
31.Warranty Remedy. The warranty does not cover adjustments required due to settling of your home. We
do not cover change in color or damage to the cabinet finish due to exposure to environmental factors
such as natural and artificial light or moisture. Due to the natural variations in wood grains, the
possibility of tone and shading differences from piece to piece is normal and is not considered defective.

rate will be multiplied by the number of years you have used the product to calculate the usage value
for offset purposes. In the unlikely event of a product defect, we will work diligently to address any
